232.txt roommates

Line 94
Room 1 becomes A, F, G. A's satisfaction: F (4) + G (5) = 9 (down from 10). D moves to Room 2: B, E, D. D's satisfaction: B (2) + E (5) =7 (up from 6). B's satisfaction in Room 2: E (5) + D (4) =9 (same as before). E's satisfaction: B (4) + D (4) =8 (up from7). F's satisfaction in Room1: A (4) + G (1) =5 (down from7). So E's satisfaction increases, D's increases, but F's decreases and A's decreases. Since both D and E would prefer the switch, but A and F would not. However, stability requires that no pair can switch to improve both or one without harming the other. In this case, D and E could potentially benefit, but F and A lose. So this swap is not possible.

Line 486
Ethan in Room3: Bob, Charlie, Ethan. Ethan's satisfaction: Bob(4) + Charlie(3)=7. Previously Ethan had5. So Ethan improves, Bob's satisfaction: Ethan(5) + Charlie(2)=7. Previously Bob had6. So Bob's satisfaction increases from6 to7. Charlie's satisfaction: Bob(3) + Ethan(3)=6. Same as before. So swapping Ethan and Ian would increase Bob's satisfaction from6 to7 and Ethan's from5 to7. However, Ian's satisfaction decreases from6 to5. So this swap would not benefit both. Since Ian's satisfaction decreases, he wouldn't agree to swap unless he's forced. But the definition of unstable is if there exists a pair who can switch to increase both, or one increases while the other stays same. In this case, Ethan and Ian swapping leads to Ethan and Bob improving, but Ian's decreases. So this doesn't qualify as an unstable pair because not both improve and not one improves with other same.
